
Session altered.


Specify the Report Type
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
AWR reports can be generated in the following formats.	Please enter the
name of the format at the prompt.  Default value is 'html'.

'html'		HTML format (default)
'text'		Text format
'active-html'	Includes Performance Hub active report

Enter value for report_type: 
Type Specified:  html



Specify the location of AWR Data
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
AWR PDB reports can be generated using data stored in this PDB or ROOT.
Please enter the desired location at the prompt. Default value is 'AWR_PDB'.

    AWR_PDB  - Use AWR data from PDB
    AWR_ROOT - Use AWR data from ROOT
Enter value for awr_location: 
Location of AWR Data Specified: AWR_PDB

Specify the number of days of snapshots to choose from
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Entering the number of days (n) will result in the most recent
(n) days of snapshots being listed.  Pressing <return> without
specifying a number lists all completed snapshots.


Enter value for num_days: 
Listing the last day's Completed Snapshots

<a class="awr" name="1500"></a>
<h3 class="awr">Time Model Statistics</h3>
<ul>
<li class="awr">DB Time represents total time in user calls</li>
<li class="awr">DB CPU represents CPU time of foreground processes</li>
<li class="awr">Total CPU Time represents foreground and background processes</li>
<li class="awr">Statistics including the word "background" measure background process
   time, therefore do not contribute to the DB time statistic</li>
<li class="awr">Ordered by % of DB time in descending order, followed by Statistic Name</li>
</ul>

</h2>
<ul>
<li class="awr"><a class="awr" href="#9000">Replication System Resource Usage</a></li>

<h3 class="awr">Top SQL with Top Events</h3>
<ul>
<li class="awr"> Top SQL statements by DB Time along with the top events by DB Time	 for those SQLs. </li>
<li class="awr"> % Activity is the percentage of DB Time due to the SQL. </li>
<li class="awr"> % Event is the percentage of DB Time due to the event that the SQL is	   waiting on. </li>
<li class="awr"> % Row Source is the percentage of DB Time due to the row source for the     SQL waiting on the event. </li>
<li class="awr"> Executions is the number of executions of the SQL that were sampled in ASH.</li>
</ul>

<pre>
	  ADDM Report for Task &apos;ADDM:3457782515_1_9&apos;
	  ------------------------------------------

Analysis Period
---------------
AWR snapshot range from 8 to 9.
Time period starts at 19-OCT-26 05.00.58 PM
Time period ends at 19-OCT-26 05.05.40 PM

Analysis Target
---------------
Database &apos;FREE&apos; with DB ID 3457782515.
Database version 23.0.0.0.0.
ADDM performed an analysis of instance FREE, numbered 1 and hosted at
8512df493b78.
ADDM detected that the system is a PDB.

Activity During the Analysis Period
-----------------------------------
Total database time was 582 seconds.
The average number of active sessions was 2.06.

Summary of Findings
-------------------
   Description			    Active Sessions	 Recommendations
				    Percent of Activity
   -------------------------------  -------------------  ---------------
1  Top SQL Statements		    1.1 | 53.12 	 1
2  PL/SQL Execution		    .84 | 40.62 	 1

4  Hard Parse			    .32 | 15.42 	 0
5  CPU Usage			    .26 | 12.44 	 2
6  Soft Parse			    .08 | 3.67		 2


~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~


	  Findings and Recommendations
	  ----------------------------

Finding 1: Top SQL Statements
Impact is 1.1 active sessions, 53.12% of total activity.
--------------------------------------------------------
SQL statements consuming significant database time were found. These
statements offer a good opportunity for performance improvement.

   Recommendation 1: SQL Tuning
   Estimated benefit is 1.1 active sessions, 53.12% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Run SQL Tuning Advisor on the SELECT statement with SQL_ID
      &quot;1ppfr2kwym0kf&quot;.
      Related Object
	 SQL statement with SQL_ID 1ppfr2kwym0kf.
	 SELECT COUNT(*), NVL(SUM(charge_amt), 0) FROM TELCO.cdr_usage WHERE
	 msisdn = :m AND call_start &gt;= SYSDATE - 1/24
   Rationale
      The SQL spent 100% of its database time on CPU, I/O and Cluster waits.
      This part of database time may be improved by the SQL Tuning Advisor.
   Rationale
      Database time for this SQL was divided as follows: 100% for SQL
      execution, 0% for parsing, 0% for PL/SQL execution and 0% for Java
      execution.
   Rationale
      SQL statement with SQL_ID &quot;1ppfr2kwym0kf&quot; was executed 2734 times and
      had an average elapsed time of 0.061 seconds.


Finding 2: PL/SQL Execution
Impact is .84 active sessions, 40.62% of total activity.
--------------------------------------------------------
PL/SQL execution consumed significant database time.

   Recommendation 1: SQL Tuning
   Estimated benefit is .84 active sessions, 40.62% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Tune the entry point PL/SQL &quot;TELCO_LOAD.TELCO_WORKLOAD.RUN_SESSION&quot; of
      type &quot;PACKAGE&quot; and ID 73149. Refer to the PL/SQL documentation for
      addition information.
   Rationale
      Subprogam ID 29 of PL/SQL ID 9131 spent 163 seconds in execution.


Finding 3: Resource Manager CPU Throttling
Impact is .41 active sessions, 19.76% of total activity.
--------------------------------------------------------
CPU Resource Manager is enabled and database sessions were found waiting for
CPU.

   Recommendation 1: Database Configuration
   Estimated benefit is .41 active sessions, 19.76% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Session CPU consumption was throttled by the Oracle Resource Manager.
      Consider revising the resource plan that was active during the analysis
      period.

   Recommendation 2: Database Configuration
   Estimated benefit is .41 active sessions, 19.76% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Consider increasing the CPU quota for this PDB. This action must be done
      in the CDB root.

   Symptoms That Led to the Finding:
   ---------------------------------
      Wait class &quot;Scheduler&quot; was consuming significant database time.
      Impact is .41 active sessions, 19.76% of total activity.


Finding 4: Hard Parse
Impact is .32 active sessions, 15.42% of total activity.
--------------------------------------------------------
Hard parsing of SQL statements was consuming significant database time.
Hard parses due to cursor environment mismatch were not consuming significant
database time.
Hard parsing SQL statements that encountered parse errors was not consuming
significant database time.
Hard parses due to literal usage and cursor invalidation were not consuming
significant database time.

   No recommendations are available.


Finding 5: CPU Usage
Impact is .26 active sessions, 12.44% of total activity.
--------------------------------------------------------
Time spent on CPU by the DB on this instance was responsible for a substantial
part of database time.

   Recommendation 1: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is 1.81 active sessions, 87.5% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Look at the &quot;Top SQL Statements&quot; finding for SQL statements consuming
      significant time on CPU. For example, the SELECT statement with SQL_ID
      &quot;1ppfr2kwym0kf&quot; is responsible for 60% of CPU usage during the analysis
      period.

   Recommendation 2: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is .26 active sessions, 12.44% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Parsing SQL statements were consuming significant CPU. Please refer to
      other findings in this task about parsing for further details.


Finding 6: Soft Parse
Impact is .08 active sessions, 3.67% of total activity.
-------------------------------------------------------
Soft parsing of SQL statements was consuming significant database time.

   Recommendation 1: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is .08 active sessions, 3.67% of total activity.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Investigate application logic to keep open the frequently used cursors.
      Note that cursors are closed by both cursor close calls and session
      disconnects.

   Recommendation 2: Database Configuration
   Estimated benefit is .08 active sessions, 3.67% of total activity.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Consider increasing the session cursor cache size by increasing the
      value of parameter &quot;session_cached_cursors&quot;.
   Rationale
      The value of parameter &quot;session_cached_cursors&quot; was &quot;50&quot; during the
      analysis period.

Finding 3: Hard Parse Due to Literal Usage
Impact is .3 active sessions, 23.7% of total activity.
------------------------------------------------------
SQL statements were not shared due to the usage of literals. This resulted in
additional hard parses which were consuming significant database time.

   Recommendation 1: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is .3 active sessions, 23.7% of total activity.
   -----------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Investigate application logic for possible use of bind variables instead
      of literals.
   Action
      Alternatively, you may set the parameter &quot;cursor_sharing&quot; to &quot;force&quot;.
   Rationale
      At least 59 SQL statements with FORCE_MATCHING_SIGNATURE
      14010260740968950482 and PLAN_HASH_VALUE 2746544118 were found to be
      using literals. An example is SQL statement with SQL_ID &quot;1757pb5rp40ft&quot;.
      Related Object
	 SQL statement with SQL_ID 1757pb5rp40ft.
	 SELECT msisdn, tariff_code, status FROM TELCO.subscriber WHERE
	 subscriber_id = 2431
   Rationale
      At least 59 SQL statements with FORCE_MATCHING_SIGNATURE
      14010260740968950482 and PLAN_HASH_VALUE 2746544118 were found to be
      using literals. An example is SQL statement with SQL_ID &quot;a4zak5905p5f2&quot;.
      Related Object
	 SQL statement with SQL_ID a4zak5905p5f2.


   Symptoms That Led to the Finding:
   ---------------------------------
      Hard parsing of SQL statements was consuming significant database time.
      Impact is .3 active sessions, 23.71% of total activity.


Finding 4: CPU Usage
Impact is .28 active sessions, 22.08% of total activity.
--------------------------------------------------------
Time spent on CPU by the DB on this instance was responsible for a substantial
part of database time.

   Recommendation 1: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is 1.25 active sessions, 98.41% of total activity.
   --------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Look at the &quot;Top SQL Statements&quot; finding for SQL statements consuming
      significant time on CPU. For example, the SELECT statement with SQL_ID
      &quot;1ppfr2kwym0kf&quot; is responsible for 30% of CPU usage during the analysis
      period.

   Recommendation 2: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is .28 active sessions, 22.08% of total activity.
   -------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Parsing SQL statements were consuming significant CPU. Please refer to
      other findings in this task about parsing for further details.


Finding 5: Soft Parse
Impact is .08 active sessions, 6.08% of total activity.
-------------------------------------------------------
Soft parsing of SQL statements was consuming significant database time.

   Recommendation 1: Application Analysis
   Estimated benefit is .08 active sessions, 6.08% of total activity.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Investigate application logic to keep open the frequently used cursors.
      Note that cursors are closed by both cursor close calls and session
      disconnects.

   Recommendation 2: Database Configuration
   Estimated benefit is .08 active sessions, 6.08% of total activity.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   Action
      Consider increasing the session cursor cache size by increasing the
      value of parameter &quot;session_cached_cursors&quot;.
   Rationale
      The value of parameter &quot;session_cached_cursors&quot; was &quot;50&quot; during the
      analysis period.
